                                        <ul class="m-0 flex list-none flex-wrap items-center p-0 short-form__author-sm gap-5">

                                                    </svg>
                                                </button>
                                            </li>
                                            <li>
                                                <button type="button" class="flex appearance-none items-center justify-center rounded-[50px] border-0 bg-transparent p-0 transition-colors cursor-pointer short-form__author-sm-item h-12 w-12 text-teal hover:text-anthracite focus:text-anthracite" aria-label="Link copied to clipboard!">
                                                    <svg class="h-[30px] w-[30px]" viewBox="0 0 24 24" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false">
                                                        <path fill="currentColor" d="M10 17.55l-1.77 1.72a2.47 2.47 0 01-3.5-3.5l4.54-4.55a2.46 2.46 0 013.39-.09l.12.1a1 1 0 001.4-1.43 2.75 2.75 0 00-.18-.21 4.46 4.46 0 00-6.09.22l-4.6 4.55a4.48 4.48 0 006.33 6.33L11.37 19A1 1 0 0010 17.55zM20.69 3.31a4.49 4.49 0 00-6.33 0L12.63 5A1 1 0 0014 6.45l1.73-1.72a2.47 2.47 0 013.5 3.5l-4.54 4.55a2.46 2.46 0 01-3.39.09l-.12-.1a1 1 0 00-1.4 1.43 2.75 2.75 0 00.23.21 4.47 4.47 0 006.09-.22l4.55-4.55a4.49 4.49 0 00.04-6.33z"></path>
                                                    </svg>
                                                </button>
                                            </li>
                                            <li>
                                                <button type="button" class="flex appearance-none items-center justify-center rounded-[50px] border-0 bg-transparent p-0 transition-colors cursor-pointer short-form__author-sm-item h-12 w-12 text-teal hover:text-anthracite focus:text-anthracite" aria-label="Print article">
                                                    <svg class="h-[30px] w-[30px]" viewBox="0 0 120 120" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false">
                                                        <g fill="currentColor" fill-rule="evenodd" transform="translate(10 14)">
                                                            <path fill-rule="nonzero" d="M93.2 26.4V60l-.1 5.4.1-1c-.1 1-.3 1.8-.7 2.6l.4-.9c-.3.6-.5 1.1-.9 1.6l-.2.4c-.4.4.5-.7.1-.3l-.6.7-.6.6c-.4.4.7-.5.2-.2l-.4.3-1.5.8.8-.3c-.8.3-1.7.5-2.5.6h.9-13.8l3.4 3.4v-25c0-2-1.6-3.5-3.4-3.5H25.7a3.5 3.5 0 00-3.4 3.4v25l3.4-3.3H11.9h.9c-.9 0-1.7-.3-2.5-.6l.8.3-1.6-.8-.3-.3c-.5-.3.6.6.2.2l-.7-.6-.6-.7.2.3c0-.2-.2-.3-.3-.4L7.2 66l.3.9a10 10 0 01-.6-2.5l.1.9c-.2-1.6 0-3.2 0-4.7V25.2l-.1.9c0-.9.3-1.7.6-2.5l-.3.8L8 23l.3-.4c.3-.5-.6.7-.2.2l.6-.6.7-.6c.4-.4-.7.5-.2.1l.3-.2 1.6-.9-.8.4c.8-.3 1.6-.6 2.5-.7l-1 .1h76.5-1c1 0 1.7.3 2.5.6l-.8-.4 1.6.9.4.2c.4.4-.7-.5-.3-.1l.7.6.6.6c.4.4-.5-.7-.2-.2l.3.4.8 1.5-.3-.8.7 2.5-.2-1v1.3c0 1.8 1.6 3.5 3.5 3.4 1.8 0 3.4-1.5 3.4-3.4 0-6-4.6-11.8-10.8-12.7l-3.9-.3h-72c-4.6 0-8.7 2.2-11.2 6C.4 21.9 0 24.8 0 27.7V64c0 2.3.6 4.5 1.8 6.5a13.3 13.3 0 0012 6.5h11.8c1.9 0 3.5-1.5 3.5-3.4v-25l-3.5 3.3h48.7L71 48.5v25.1c0 1.9 1.6 3.4 3.4 3.4h12.3c3.8 0 7.7-1.5 10.2-4.5 2.1-2.5 3.2-5.5 3.2-8.8V26.5c0-1.8-1.6-3.5-3.4-3.4a3.4 3.4 0 00-3.5 3.3z"></path>

                                                            <path fill-rule="nonzero" d="M19 52h62c1.8 0 3.5-1.6 3.5-3.5-.1-1.8-1.5-3.4-3.5-3.4H19.1c-1.9 0-3.6 1.6-3.5 3.4 0 1.9 1.5 3.4 3.4 3.4zm54.4-15.8H81c1.8 0 3.5-1.6 3.5-3.4-.1-1.9-1.5-3.4-3.5-3.4h-7.5c-1.8 0-3.5 1.5-3.4 3.4 0 1.8 1.5 3.4 3.4 3.4z"></path>
                                                            <rect width="25" height="6" x="38" y="58" rx="3"></rect>

                            <div class="m-0 p-0 [&amp;_a:not(.btn)]:cursor-pointer [&amp;_a:not(.btn)]:text-start [&amp;_a:not(.btn)]:tracking-normal [&amp;_a:not(.btn)]:text-teal [&amp;_blockquote]:font-medium [&amp;_blockquote]:border-l-4 [&amp;_blockquote]:border-teal [&amp;_blockquote]:pl-6 [&amp;_blockquote]:py-1 [&amp;_blockquote]:border-y-0 [&amp;_blockquote]:border-r-0 [&amp;_blockquote_p]:m-0 [&amp;_li_blockquote]:my-0 [&amp;_li_blockquote]:border-0 [&amp;_li_blockquote]:p-0 [&amp;_li_blockquote]:pl-0 [&amp;_li_blockquote_p]:inline [&amp;_h3]:mt-(--heading-margin) [&amp;_h3]:tracking-normal [&amp;_h3]:text-(length:--font-size-h3) [&amp;_h3]:leading-(--leading-h3) [&amp;_h3]:font-bold [&amp;_h3]:text-anthracite [&amp;_h4]:mt-(--heading-margin) [&amp;_h4]:tracking-normal [&amp;_h4]:text-(length:--font-size-h4) [&amp;_h4]:leading-(--leading-h4) [&amp;_h4]:font-bold [&amp;_h4]:text-anthracite [&amp;_img]:mt-(--heading-margin) [&amp;_img]:h-auto [&amp;_img]:max-w-full [&amp;_table]:my-5 [&amp;_table]:border-collapse max-tablet:[&amp;_table]:!w-auto [&amp;_th]:bg-teal [&amp;_th]:px-2.5 [&amp;_th]:py-2.5 [&amp;_th]:text-start [&amp;_th]:font-bold [&amp;_th]:text-white [&amp;_td]:border-b [&amp;_td]:border-anthracite [&amp;_td]:px-2.5 [&amp;_td]:py-2.5 [&amp;_table.table--secondary_th]:bg-blue [&amp;_.rtf-table-container.table--secondary_th]:bg-blue [&amp;_.rtf-table-container.rtf-table--no-lines_td]:border-b-0 [&amp;_.rtf-table-container.rtf-table--no-lines_td]:p-inherit [&amp;_.rtf-table--no-lines_td]:border-b-0 [&amp;_.rtf-table--no-lines_td]:p-inherit [&amp;_ol]:my-[1em] [&amp;_ol]:ps-10 [&amp;_ol]:tracking-normal [&amp;_ol]:text-(length:--font-size-copy-large) [&amp;_ol]:leading-(--leading-copy-large) [&amp;_ol]:text-gray-600 [&amp;_p]:mt-(--heading-margin) [&amp;_p]:max-w-[70ch] [&amp;_p]:tracking-normal [&amp;_p]:text-(length:--font-size-copy-large) [&amp;_p]:leading-(--leading-copy-large) [&amp;_p]:text-gray-600 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)]:my-[1em] [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)]:list-none [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)]:ps-10 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)]:tracking-normal [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)]:text-(length:--font-size-copy-large) [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)]:leading-(--leading-copy-large) [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)]:text-gray-600 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li]:list-none [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::marker]:content-none [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:mr-[1em] desktop:[&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:mr-[1.1em] [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:ms-[-1.6em] [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:inline-block [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:h-[0.5em] [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:w-[0.5em] [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:bg-teal [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li::before]:content-[&#x27;&#x27;] [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_p]:inline [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h2]:mt-0 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h2]:inline-block [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h2]:align-middle [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h3]:mt-0 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h3]:inline-block [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h3]:align-middle [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h4]:mt-0 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h4]:inline-block [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h4]:align-middle [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h5]:mt-0 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h5]:inline-block [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h5]:align-middle [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h6]:mt-0 [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h6]:inline-block [&amp;_ul:not(.form__tags-content)_li_h6]:align-middle [&amp;&gt;:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;h2:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;h3:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;h4:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;p:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;figure:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;img:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;ol:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;ul:first-child]:mt-0 [&amp;_p]:!max-w-none [&amp;_p]:!text-[18px] wide:[&amp;_p]:!text-(length:--font-size-copy-large)">
                                <div class="ck-content">
                                    <p>
                                        Anthropic is planning an IPO this fall.
                                        <a href="#_ftn1" name="_ftnref1" title="">
                                            <span style="font-family:&quot;Aptos&quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;">[1]</span>
                                        </a>
                                        In an unconventional approach to pre-IPO corporate communications, Evan Hubinger of Anthropic tweeted on September 8 that:
                                        <a href="#_ftn2" name="_ftnref2" title="">
                                            <span style="font-family:&quot;Aptos&quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;">[2]</span>
                                        </a>
                                    </p>
                                    <p style="margin-left:.5in;">
                                        <i>… we really do earnestly believe AI could kill all humans!&nbsp;</i>
                                    </p>
                                    <p>Given these high stakes, how should we think about Anthropic’s IPO? The implicit argument is that AI is so dangerous that Anthropic must raise vast sums of money to safely develop an AI that does not lead to human extinction. The AI singularity is coming whether we like it or not, and thus we need guardrails prudently installed by Anthropic. This argument is ESG on steroids: Extinction, Singularity, Guardrails.</p>
                                    <p>Jones (2026), in a balanced discussion, provides the following comparison for the upsides and downsides of AI:</p>
                                    <p style="margin-left:.5in;">
                                        <i>In anticipation of the first test of the atomic bomb, the scientists of the Manhattan Project faced potentially catastrophic risks: for example, what if the nuclear chain reaction continued unabated, igniting the atmosphere and potentially killing all life on Earth? … They estimated the probability to be extremely low, and the Trinity test went forward. But how large would the risk have to be to avert the test?</i>
                                    </p>
                                    <p style="margin-left:.5in;">
                                        <i>… Suppose on one side that AI has incredible benefits, raising economic growth to 10 &nbsp;percent per year and thus doubling the average standard of living every seven years. However, it comes with a one-time risk of killing everyone on the planet.&nbsp;</i>
                                    </p>
                                    <p>Anthropic’s IPO is like Robert Oppenheimer doing an IPO for the Manhattan Project in 1945.</p>
                                    <p>Whether Anthropic is proceeding wisely is a complicated issue with valid arguments on both sides. Instead of tackling this thorny dilemma, let me ask the following narrow question: if you have a company whose operations may cause death and destruction, does the danger increase if that company has an IPO?&nbsp;</p>
                                    <p>The good news is that there are reasons to believe that public firms act safely compared to private firms because public firms face more scrutiny and have more financial resources to spend on safety. The bad news is that as an empirical matter, public firms sometimes have more workplace accidents than private firms.&nbsp;</p>
                                    <p>Let’s consider one industry that poses many safety concerns: coal mining. Gilje and Wittry (2021) study U.S. coal mines from 1985 to 2018, and find that:</p>
                                    <p style="margin-left:.5in;">
                                        <i>… workplace safety is strongly related to listing status. Specifically, mines that are purchased by public firms or are part of an IPO experience a large increase in safety violations. However, this increase is concentrated in what appears to be tail risk, or violations for potential accidents stemming from low-probability events. Such violations increase by nearly 57%. Furthermore, upon a listing status change, workplace fatalities increase four-fold relative to the unconditional sample mean.</i>
                                    </p>
                                    <p>
                                        We can also consider specific IPOs of coal mining firms. International Coal Group (ICG) had an IPO on December 12, 2005. Three weeks later, its Sago Mine in West Virginia exploded, killing 12 miners. Like Anthropic, ICG was touted as being especially safety conscious. Subsequent lawsuits alleged that ICG “wrongly claimed in its initial public offering for stock purchasers that its operations had a good safety record.”
                                        <a href="#_ftn3" name="_ftnref3" title="">
                                            <span style="font-family:&quot;Aptos&quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;">[3]</span>
                                        </a>
                                    </p>
                                    <p>
                                        A more striking case involves the Ulyanovskaya mine disaster in 2007. In preparation for an IPO, the Russian firm Yuzhkuzbassugol brought an outside auditor to inspect its supposedly safe and modern mine in Siberia.
                                        <a href="#_ftn4" name="_ftnref4" title="">
                                            <span style="font-family:&quot;Aptos&quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;">[4]</span>
                                        </a>
                                        The auditor, along with senior mining executives, went underground to observe a demonstration of the mine’s “advanced mining safety system.” A massive methane explosion occurred, killing more than 100 people including the auditor and the management team.
                                    </p>
                                    <p>What lessons can we learn from the Ulyanovskaya disaster? First, just because management thinks that something is safe, that doesn’t make it safe. Second, even when management is aware of the risk and devotes considerable resources to reducing it, catastrophe can still occur.&nbsp;</p>
                                    <p>
                                        I’ve <a href="https://www.acadian-asset.com/investment-insights/owenomics/waiting-for-the-ipo-wave">previously discussed</a>
                                        many aspects of IPOs: whether you should buy them, why a company might do one, and why they occur during stock market bubbles. The Pets.com IPO of February 2000 is now widely seen as marking the beginning of the collapse of the tech stock bubble. Let’s hope that future historians do not look back at the Anthropic IPO and see it as marking the beginning of the collapse of human civilization.&nbsp;
                                    </p>
                                    <p>&nbsp;</p>
                                    <hr/>

                                <div class="tablet:mx-[30px] tablet:w-full">
                                    <h3 class="mt-4 text-(length:--font-size-copy-large) leading-(--leading-copy-large) font-bold text-anthracite tablet:mt-0">Owen A. Lamont, Ph.D.</h3>
                                    <span class="block text-(length:--font-size-copy-large) leading-(--leading-copy-large) font-medium text-anthracite">Senior Vice President, Portfolio Manager, Research</span>
                                    <p class="mt-4 text-(length:--font-size-copy) leading-[1.5] text-gray-600">Owen joined the Acadian investment team in 2023. In addition to more than 20 years of experience in asset management as a researcher and portfolio manager, Owen has been a member of the faculty at Harvard University, Princeton University, The University of Chicago Graduate School of Business, and Yale School of Management. His professional and academic focus is behavioral finance, and he has published papers on short selling, stock returns, and investor behavior in leading academic journals, and he has testified before the U.S. House of Representatives and the U.S. Senate. Owen earned a Ph.D. in economics from the Massachusetts Institute of Technology and a B.A. in economics and government from Oberlin College.</p>

            self.__next_f.push([1, "\u003cdiv class=\"ck-content\"\u003e\u003cp\u003eAnthropic is planning an IPO this fall.\u003ca href=\"#_ftn1\" name=\"_ftnref1\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[1]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e In an unconventional approach to pre-IPO corporate communications, Evan Hubinger of Anthropic tweeted on September 8 that:\u003ca href=\"#_ftn2\" name=\"_ftnref2\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[2]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"margin-left:.5in;\"\u003e\u003ci\u003e… we really do earnestly believe AI could kill all humans!\u0026nbsp;\u003c/i\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eGiven these high stakes, how should we think about Anthropic’s IPO? The implicit argument is that AI is so dangerous that Anthropic must raise vast sums of money to safely develop an AI that does not lead to human extinction. The AI singularity is coming whether we like it or not, and thus we need guardrails prudently installed by Anthropic. This argument is ESG on steroids: Extinction, Singularity, Guardrails.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eJones (2026), in a balanced discussion, provides the following comparison for the upsides and downsides of AI:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"margin-left:.5in;\"\u003e\u003ci\u003eIn anticipation of the first test of the atomic bomb, the scientists of the Manhattan Project faced potentially catastrophic risks: for example, what if the nuclear chain reaction continued unabated, igniting the atmosphere and potentially killing all life on Earth? … They estimated the probability to be extremely low, and the Trinity test went forward. But how large would the risk have to be to avert the test?\u003c/i\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"margin-left:.5in;\"\u003e\u003ci\u003e… Suppose on one side that AI has incredible benefits, raising economic growth to 10\u0026nbsp;percent per year and thus doubling the average standard of living every seven years. However, it comes with a one-time risk of killing everyone on the planet.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/i\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAnthropic’s IPO is like Robert Oppenheimer doing an IPO for the Manhattan Project in 1945.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhether Anthropic is proceeding wisely is a complicated issue with valid arguments on both sides. Instead of tackling this thorny dilemma, let me ask the following narrow question: if you have a company whose operations may cause death and destruction, does the danger increase if that company has an IPO?\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe good news is that there are reasons to believe that public firms act safely compared to private firms because public firms face more scrutiny and have more financial resources to spend on safety. The bad news is that as an empirical matter, public firms sometimes have more workplace accidents than private firms.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eLet’s consider one industry that poses many safety concerns: coal mining. Gilje and Wittry (2021) study U.S. coal mines from 1985 to 2018, and find that:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"margin-left:.5in;\"\u003e\u003ci\u003e… workplace safety is strongly related to listing status. Specifically, mines that are purchased by public firms or are part of an IPO experience a large increase in safety violations. However, this increase is concentrated in what appears to be tail risk, or violations for potential accidents stemming from low-probability events. Such violations increase by nearly 57%. Furthermore, upon a listing status change, workplace fatalities increase four-fold relative to the unconditional sample mean.\u003c/i\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWe can also consider specific IPOs of coal mining firms. International Coal Group (ICG) had an IPO on December 12, 2005. Three weeks later, its Sago Mine in West Virginia exploded, killing 12 miners. Like Anthropic, ICG was touted as being especially safety conscious. Subsequent lawsuits alleged that ICG “wrongly claimed in its initial public offering for stock purchasers that its operations had a good safety record.”\u003ca href=\"#_ftn3\" name=\"_ftnref3\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[3]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eA more striking case involves the Ulyanovskaya mine disaster in 2007. In preparation for an IPO, the Russian firm Yuzhkuzbassugol brought an outside auditor to inspect its supposedly safe and modern mine in Siberia.\u003ca href=\"#_ftn4\" name=\"_ftnref4\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:12.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[4]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e The auditor, along with senior mining executives, went underground to observe a demonstration of the mine’s “advanced mining safety system.” A massive methane explosion occurred, killing more than 100 people including the auditor and the management team.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhat lessons can we learn from the Ulyanovskaya disaster? First, just because management thinks that something is safe, that doesn’t make it safe. Second, even when management is aware of the risk and devotes considerable resources to reducing it, catastrophe can still occur.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI’ve \u003ca href=\"https://www.acadian-asset.com/investment-insights/owenomics/waiting-for-the-ipo-wave\"\u003epreviously discussed\u003c/a\u003e many aspects of IPOs: whether you should buy them, why a company might do one, and why they occur during stock market bubbles. The Pets.com IPO of February 2000 is now widely seen as marking the beginning of the collapse of the tech stock bubble. Let’s hope that future historians do not look back at the Anthropic IPO and see it as marking the beginning of the collapse of human civilization.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch4\u003eEndnotes\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cdiv\u003e\u003cdiv id=\"ftn1\"\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"#_ftnref1\" name=\"_ftn1\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:10.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[1]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e References to this and other companies should not be interpreted as recommendations to buy or sell specific securities. Acadian and/or the author of this post may hold positions in one or more securities associated with these companies.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\u003cdiv id=\"ftn2\"\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"#_ftnref2\" name=\"_ftn2\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:10.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[2]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e \u003ca href=\"https://x.com/EvanHub/status/2097497037956891126?s=20\"\u003ehttps://x.com/EvanHub/status/2097497037956891126?s=20\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\u003cdiv id=\"ftn3\"\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"#_ftnref3\" name=\"_ftn3\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:10.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[3]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e “Union says ICG Misled Investors,” \u003ci\u003eThe Wall Street Journal\u003c/i\u003e, June 18, 2008.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\u003cdiv id=\"ftn4\"\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"#_ftnref4\" name=\"_ftn4\" title=\"\"\u003e\u003cspan style=\"font-family:\u0026quot;Aptos\u0026quot;,sans-serif;font-size:10.0pt;line-height:115%;\"\u003e[4]\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e \u003ca href=\"http://www.mineaccidents.com.au/mine-accident/226/ulyanovskaya-mine\"\u003e\"Ulyanovskaya mine,\"\u003c/a\u003e \u003ci\u003eMine Accidents and Disasters\u003c/i\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\u003ch4\u003eReferences\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003eGilje, Erik P., and Michael D. Wittry. \u003ca href=\"https://www.nber.org/system/files/working_papers/w28798/w28798.pdf?utm_source=PANTHEON_STRIPPED\"\u003e\u003ci\u003eIs public equity deadly? Evidence from workplace safety and productivity tradeoffs in the coal industry\u003c/i\u003e.\u003c/a\u003e No. w28798. National Bureau of Economic Research, 2021.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eJones, Charles I. \"\u003ca href=\"https://www-leland.stanford.edu/~chadj/AIandEconomicFuture.pdf\"\u003eAI and Our Economic Future\u003c/a\u003e.\"\u0026nbsp;\u003ci\u003eJournal of Economic Perspectives\u003c/i\u003e, vol. 40(3), pages 3-22, Summer 2026.\u003cbr /\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e"])
        </script>
